People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm in the right place for this. 

I came in because... well, I guess I've started doing some things at work—I do bookkeeping—and at school that just don't make a lot of sense lately. It's probably nothing too serious, really, just some silly habits, you know? Like, instead of filing, I'll start lining receipts all the way across the floor. Or I'll catch myself tapping the calculator in sets of nine. Just little quirks... is that the right kind of thing to tell you? 

I'm a little embarrassed to admit it, but sometimes I'll also just get stuck standing by the copier with my hands held up in the air, and I won't put them down until someone asks if I'm okay. I know it sounds really strange, and I don't want you to think badly of me... I just don't want to cause any trouble at my job. Does that make sense? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, stop doing them? I... I think so? I mean, I haven't really tried to just force myself to stop, because, well, like I said, I didn't really realize it was a problem until people started looking at me funny. 

But I'm sure if I really concentrated, I could just... not do it. I mean, it's just silly stuff, right? I came in because I know it doesn't really make sense, and I want to fix it. I definitely don't want to be a disruption at work or at school. I'm trying really hard to be a good employee and student. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... a pattern? Well... yeah, I guess so. I'm a little embarrassed to admit this, because I really don't want you to think I'm weird or anything. But... sometimes at work, I get, well, convinced that certain invoice totals, or account numbers, and even the due dates are... like, messages about me. Like a pattern I'm supposed to figure out. 

I know it sounds really strange when I say it out loud. My supervisor actually sat down and showed me that they are just normal accounting entries, and I totally agreed with him because I want to be a good employee and not cause any problems. But... even after he showed me, deep down, I still kind of feel like they are tracking whether I understand the pattern. 

I'm trying really hard not to think that way, though. Does that make sense? I hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying that. I just want to be completely honest with you so I can fix it and do a better job.

It's honestly just something I catch myself doing. \n\nLike I said, it's really not a huge issue, and I definitely don't want you to think I'm doing it because I'm stressed or can't handle my job or anything like that. I'm actually doing fine there, I promise. It's just... I just kind of do it without thinking about it at all. \n\nAm I making sense? I really want to make sure I'm explaining this the right way so I'm not making your job harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself preferring things to be symmetrical or perfectly arranged in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not especially. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around all day making sure everything is perfectly straight. It's just... normal neatness, I guess? \n\nDoes that sound alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're doing these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I definitely feel in control. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I guess... yeah, it kind of goes back to what I mentioned a little bit ago. I'm really sorry to bring it up again, because I know how strange it sounds, and I really don't want you to think I'm... you know, completely out of touch with reality or anything. 

But... yeah, it's that feeling about the invoice totals and the account numbers and due dates. I just get so convinced that they are actual messages about me. And even when my supervisor takes the time to show me that they are just normal accounting entries—and I always agree with him, because I really do want to be a good employee and do things right—I still can't entirely shake it. Deep down, I still feel like they are tracking whether I understand the pattern. 

I know it's a really unusual thing to believe, and I'm trying so hard to push those thoughts away so I don't cause any problems at work. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question right... I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need so I can get better.

I really hope I'm not making this sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the patterns or habits you're experiencing are related to any thoughts or beliefs that you hold to be true, even if they might seem unusual or unexplainable to others?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm really sorry to bring it up again, because I know how strange it sounds, and I really don't want you to think I'm... you know, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nBut... yeah, it's that feeling about the invoice totals and the account numbers and due dates.
